s=open('LESSONS.md').read()
add0 = """
### Stage 0b: a master copy of Ferrari's fire and smoke

Hearths are where people gather, and Ferrari did paint fire: the torch on the peak in *Living Worlds*
V01. I measured it from his index map (`src/lw.py`, the willow student's loader) and copied it with
my own painters on his sky (`src/stage0_fire.py`).

![fire copy](stages/s0/s0_fire_copy.png)

**What his pixels do:**

- **The smoke is a 14-step purple ramp** whose value sits close to the sky's (mean L 48, against
  a sky of 30–60), shifted in hue rather than lighter.
- **It fills ~70% of its region.** 40% of its pixels are isolated checker pixels at the margins.
  The core lobes are solid, their upper edges a touch lighter.
- **It starts right at the flame, continuous, and billows wide.** At 60 px above the source it is
  ~45 px across.
- **The flame is a mottled clump** of yellow, orange and red pixels, leaning downwind, with single
  red-orange sparks trailing off the tips. It is never a clean shape.
- **The brazier's rim and the rock tops carry 1 px orange rims** facing the fire.

**Mine, after seven rounds:** my first smoke was random speckles; the next, a thin diagonal of
checker; then a smooth ellipse that read as a balloon. The smoke is now two octaves of smooth noise
over a widening, down-bending envelope. The density is quantised as solid, then 50% checker, then
25% sparse, with lit upper edges. The flame is teardrop tongues with a noise-mottled heat, a wind
lean, and tip sparks. **Honest comparison:** it reads as a plume now, but his has more tones inside
the lobes and broader, more sculpted billows. Mine is still a tube with blobs. His flame clump is
chunkier and less tongue-shaped than mine.
"""
s=s.replace("\n---\n\n## Stage 1: one figure, built up", add0+"\n---\n\n## Stage 1: one figure, built up")
add3 = """
![walk vs Muybridge](stages/s3/s3_walk_vs_muybridge.png)

**The walk against Muybridge** (a woman walking, one plate, six frames area-reduced to 32 px, then
thresholded to silhouettes). My first stride was timid: heel-to-toe about 0.35 of stature where his
is ~0.45, with no forward lean. The contact key now has thighs at +30° / −22°, a 4° lean, a lower
pelvis at contact and more arm swing. His frames also show what my bodies lacked: mass. They have
buttocks and a chest in profile, and a chest deeper than my first torsos (16 cm deep, when a man's
is 22–25 cm). The torso capsules are now deeper, and the Curtis fisherman comparison (a man bent
over a vertical spear in a canoe) confirmed it. His body is a heavy rounded mass over the pole; mine
was a stick.
"""
s=s.replace("**Not there yet:** the throw and squat_play poses are weak at 24 px.", add3+"\n**Not there yet:** the throw and squat_play poses are weak at 24 px.")
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)
EOF

p=open('pal.py').read()

    \"\"\"A Puget Sound shed-roof plank house seen side-on (guide 03, B1 at 10 m): a long low wall of
    silver-grey vertical planks with battens, the roof a gentle single slope, smoke seeping from a
    propped roof plank rather than a chimney. Painted flat, behind everything nearer.\"\"\"
    H, W = idx.shape
    rng = rngs(seed)
    out = idx.copy()
    base = int(round(sc.oy + z_far * math.sin(sc.cv.e) * sc.scale))
    xa = int(round(sc.ox + x0 * sc.scale))
    xb = int(round(sc.ox + x1 * sc.scale))
    pw = max(1, int(round(0.28 * sc.scale / 1.65 * 1.65 * 0.6)))      # plank width ~0.28 units
    for x in range(max(0, xa), min(W, xb)):
        f = (x - xa) / max(1, xb - xa)
        top = int(round(base - sc.scale * (height - 0.35 * f)))       # roof slopes down to the back
        for y in range(max(0, top), min(H, base)):
            k = (x - xa) // pw
            st = 2 if k % 2 == 0 else 1
            if (x - xa) % pw == 0:
                st = 0 if sc.scale >= 12 else 1                     # the seam / batten shadow
            if y == top or y == top + 1:
                st = 3 if y == top else 1                          # the roof edge catching light
            if y >= base - max(1, sc.scale // 12):
                st = 0                                              # a dark foot
            # water streaks under the eaves
            if sc.scale >= 16 and y < top + sc.scale * 0.5 and rng.random() < 0.02:
                st = max(0, st - 1)
            out[y, x] = M['PLANK'] * 8 + st
    if door is not None:
        dx = int(round(sc.ox + door * sc.scale))
        dw, dh = max(1, int(sc.scale * 0.25)), max(2, int(sc.scale * 0.75))
        out[max(0, base - dh):base, max(0, dx - dw):min(W, dx + dw)] = M['DARKB'] * 8 + 0
    if smoke:
        sx = int(round(sc.ox + (x0 + (x1 - x0) * 0.4) * sc.scale))
        sy = int(round(base - sc.scale * (height - 0.14)))
        from setting import paint_smoke
        out = paint_smoke(out, sx, sy, sc.scale, phase=phase, seed=seed + 5, drift=0.5,
                          rows=int(sc.scale * 1.3))
    return out, base


def _mix_idx(fig_idx, bg_idx):""")

# output:
## Sheets

- `sheets/A_activity_x_phase.png`: activity × phase.
- `B_age_sex_x_dress.png`: age and sex × dress.
- `C_facing_x_size.png`: 8 facings × 32→6 px.
- `D_hour_x_activity.png`: hour × activity, including night by firelight.
- `E_seed_x_group.png`: individual variation from the seed (stature ±4%, build, three skin tones,
  hair loose or tied, phase offsets).
- `F_*_distances.png`: each group at 32, 16, 8 and 5 px per person.
- `G_iso_30.png`: groups at the 30° iso view.
- `H_true_scale.png`: the small sizes at 1× and 2×.

Entry point: `person.build(spec, size_px)` → prims; `render.Scene` + `render.render_scene` for a
scene; `scenes.SCENES[name](size_px, elev, hour, phase, seed, sun)`.

---

Bash: Add technique portfolio to LESSONS
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/people && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('LESSONS.md').read()
port = """
---

## The portfolio of techniques (each with its trigger and its reference image)

Following the synthesis' advice, the method is a set of specific recipes, each with a study image as
its spec:

| technique | trigger | recipe | reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| capsule figure at target resolution | any figure | z-buffered capsules, never downsampled; forms under 0.7 px radius drawn as Bresenham runs | s4 ladder |
| pixel floors | size < 10 px | torso/skirt floors ~0.9 px radius at 6 px; head ×1.25–1.65; limbs ×1.45 | s4 ladder, C sheet |
| dark head dot | size < 10 px | hair sphere rides up, face shrinks: the top pixel is always dark | s4 ladder |
| hair frames the face | ≥ 16 px | hair sphere set back and up, face capsule forward from brow to chin, loose hair wider than the face down the back | s1 facings |
| round back | bending activities | two-segment spine, upper curl = 0.35 × pitch | s0 Bruegel bend |
| prop line through the hands | two-handed tools | arm IK to targets on the tool's line; the prop drawn through both hands | A sheet: dig, spear, dipnet, paddle, pound |
| garment swallows limbs | skirts, capes, robes | a larger capsule with a depth bias; flat hem; fringed hem by per-column cut | s2 dress |
| two families + group-blurred normals | all shading | lit (n·L > 0.04) vs shadow; normals blurred within a surface group first | s1 build-up |
| far-limb step and occlusion seam | ≥ 10 px | far-side parts −1 step; farther part −1 step where a nearer part crosses it | s1 build-up |
| edge rule | figure on any ground | silhouette pixels too close in value to the ground move one step away | F sheets at 8 px |
| contact line + sheared cast shadow | sunlit | darkest step under the feet; mask projected on the ground, checker beyond 0.35 of stature | s1, D sheet |
| firelight | night | point light from world positions; lit family warm, ambient cool; ground pool dithered | s5 fire |
| exact water mirror | water | reflect world positions about the water plane; lights −2, darks −1; dash rows break it; submerge | F weir, canoe |
| Ferrari smoke | hearths, houses | two-octave noise plume; solid core, 50% checker margins, 25% sparse ends; lit tops | s0 fire copy |
| mottled flame | hearths | teardrop tongues, noise-mottled heat, wind lean, tip sparks; hidden by nearer figures | s0 fire copy |
| calm ground | behind small figures | low-contrast ramps, 2–5% marks | s0 calm field vs noisy |
| props LOD | settings | stakes ≥ 3 px apart, dark; rails drop below 10–20 px | F weir |
"""
s=s.replace("\n---\n\n## Where it stands, honestly (first pass)", port + "\n---\n\n## Where it stands, honestly (first pass)")
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)
EOF

# output:
step = np.maximum(step, fs)
    under = n[..., 1] < -0.55
    if size_px >= 12:
        step = np.where((lam <= 0.05) & under, 0, step)
    if size_px < 10:
        # at a few pixels only the legs keep the far-side step (it is what separates them)
        legs = np.isin(cv.part, [p for p, t in cv.tags.items() if t[:4] in ('thig', 'shin', 'foot')])
        step = step + np.where(legs, cv.stepmod, 0)
        seam = False
    else:
        step = step + cv.stepmod
    # patterns
    pat = cv.pat
    H, W = idx.shape
    ys, xs = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    colhash = ((xs * 73856093) ^ (seed * 19349663)) % 7
    bark = (mat == M['BARK']) | (mat == M['HIDE']) | (mat == M['BARKT'])
    if size_px >= 14:
        # fringe: alternate strands one step down in the fringe zone
        fr = bark & (pat > 0.5) & (pat < 1.5)
        step = np.where(fr & (colhash % 3 == 0), step - 1, step)
        step = np.where(bark & (pat > 1.5), step - 1, step)       # waist band
    wool = mat == M['WOOL']
    mat = np.where(wool & (pat > 0.5) & (pat < 1.5), M['BAND'], mat)
    mat = np.where(wool & (pat > 1.5) & (pat < 2.5), M['DARKB'], mat)
    step = np.where(wool & (pat > 2.5), step - 1, step)          # vertical folds
    straw = mat == M['STRAW']
    if size_px >= 16:
        step = np.where(straw & (pat > 0.5), step - 1, step)
    # twining rows (4), fur (5): the texture inside the mass, contrast inside the mark
    step = np.where(~wool & (np.abs(pat - 4) < 0.5), step - 1, step)
    furh = ((xs * 73856093) ^ (ys * 19349663) ^ (seed * 83492791)) % 9
    step = np.where((np.abs(pat - 5) < 0.5) & (furh == 0), step + 1, step)      # lit hair tips
    step = np.where((np.abs(pat - 5) < 0.5) & (furh == 1) & ((ys % 2) == 0), step - 1, step)
    # seams: nearer part over a farther part of the same figure
    if seam:
        z = cv.z
        part = cv.part
        fig = cv.fig
        dark = np.zeros_like(m)
        thr = max(0.6, 0.02 * size_px * 1.0)
        for dy, dx in ((-1, 0), (1, 0), (0, -1), (0, 1)):
            zn = np.roll(np.roll(z, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            pn = np.roll(np.roll(part, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            fn = np.roll(np.roll(fig, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            mn = np.roll(np.roll(m, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            gn = np.roll(np.roll(cv.group, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            dark |= m & mn & (fn == fig) & (gn != cv.group) & (zn > z + thr)
        step = np.where(dark, step - 1, step)
    step = np.clip(step, 0, NSTEP - 1)
    cv.idx = np.where(m, mat * 8 + step, 0).astype(np.int16)
    return cv.idx

Bash: Write round 2 and final state in LESSONS
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/people && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('LESSONS.md').read()
a=s.index("## Where it stands, honestly (first pass)")
s=s[:a]+"""## Round 2: an outside critique, and what changed

I asked a separate critic (a fresh instance with only the images, the naturalist's guide, the
moodboard and Ferrari's scenes) for a ranked list. Its points, and what I did about each:

1. **"8 and 5 px scenes look downsampled; neighbours merge into one skin smear."** They are drawn at
   that size, but it was right about the merging. I did three things:
   - **A seam between people:** where one figure overlaps another, the farther one takes a dark
     edge, at every size.
   - **Ground marks thin out** below 12 px, so the few-pixel people are the loudest thing.
   - **The treeline becomes gentle bumps** below 12 px instead of castle battlements.

   **Still true:** at 5 px per person a camas crowd at real spacing (people ~1 m apart) is a band of
   colour cues. The sim placing fewer, more spaced people would help more than the painter can.
2. **"Skin pale and pink, breechclout a near-black smudge that reads as anatomy."** Skin is now the
   guide's warm brown with a deeper midtone. There are three tones, chosen per seed. The
   breechclout is a cedar-bark (coast) or buckskin (Plateau) flap with a belt line.
3. **"Figures sink into the sand."** The ground ramps are now low-contrast by construction, with
   2–5% marks. The edge rule already separates silhouettes.
4. **"Long checkered noon shadows read as skid marks."** Noon's sun is now at 68°, so shadows are
   short. The checker only starts beyond 0.6 of stature. Long shadows are left to morning and
   golden hour.
5. **"Seated poses are interchangeable lumps."**
   - **Weave:** the woman now reaches to a real basket on the ground in front, with arm IK to it.
   - **Sit:** arms reach round the knees, with IK to the knee joints; the IK can now target joints.
   - **Paddle:** now drawn in a canoe on water in the sheet.
   - **Sit_eat:** the legs are less splayed.
6. **"Dig reads ape-like; gather reads as crawling."**
   - **Dig:** a flat back hinged at the hips, legs straighter, both hands high on the stick,
     levering (spine curl 6° instead of 0.35 × pitch).
   - **Gather:** kneels on one knee with the trunk up, one hand reaching down ahead, the other at
     the hip basket.
7. **"Fire scene lacks firelight logic."**
   - **The light:** it sits higher (0.25) with a longer reach, so faces and chests turned to the
     fire go warm while backs stay cool.
   - **The pool:** it is irregular, with its distance warped by smooth noise, not a ring.
   - **The tender:** she moved so nobody stands in front of the flame.
   - **The smoke:** it is the Ferrari plume from stage 0b.
8. **"The tumpline is missing; carry_load is unreadable."**
   - **Tumpline:** a light cedar-bark strap from the basket to a band across the brow. Pale
     against the black hair, where the first dark strap vanished.
   - **Carry_load:** two red spawning salmon, each with a tail, one in each hand.
9. **"Dress silhouettes miss the guide's shapes."**
   - **Rain gear:** the hat has a pointed crown and a dark knob; the cape flares more.
   - **Blanket and robes:** the blanket flares and gets a slight hem. Fur robes are speckled with
     lit hair-tips and have ragged hems. The bark skirt and cape have fringed hems, a per-column cut
     at the far end of the capsule.
   - **Buckskin:** the shirt and smoked leggings separate.
   - **Basket hat:** bigger.
   - **Elders:** grey hair, a rounder stoop, softer knees.
10. **"Scenery pulls focus."**
    - **Planks** are of varied width and tone, with water streaks and a sun-bleached top; the door
      is dark plank, not black.
    - **Rack salmon** are muted toward red-brown.
    - **Weir stakes** are dark and never closer than 3 px.

What it said to protect, and I have: the carrying procession, golden hour and dusk, poles as clean
1 px runs, the ladder down to 11 px, and the notan → grey → colour discipline.

---

## Where it stands, honestly

**Reads well:**

- Single figures from 32 down to ~11 px: activity, sex, age and dress read instantly, each carried
  by one mass or one prop line.
- The carrying file, the weir, the canoe and the village work scene at 32–16 px.
- Golden hour, dusk, and night by firelight.
- The size ladder at 2×.
- Down to 6 px a figure is still a person: dark head dot, body, legs, and one colour cue (a
  blanket, a hat, a basket).

**Not there yet, and why:**

- **Beside Ferrari, these are clean sprites, not paintings.** Masses are flat within their two
  families, and the painterly layer (hue variation inside a mass, broken edges, dithered turning)
  is thin. Bruegel's figures in stage 0 are chunkier and have a dark internal shadow that ties
  their masses; mine are still a little slim and evenly lit.
- **Faces and hands are generic.** At 32 px a face is a skin capsule framed by hair, and a hand is
  the end of a forearm. That is deliberate (no caricature), but the next step up in quality is a
  1 px brow shadow and a distinct hand pixel at 32 px.
- **Seated figures at 12 px and below merge into a blob of skirt and thighs.**
- **Far crowds (5 px per person) at real spacing become bands of colour cues** (see Round 2, item
  1).
- **Settings are sketches.** The weir, rack, canoe, hearth and plank house are enough to give the
  figures context. The forest, the grounds and the water surfaces belong to the other students and
  should replace mine in any real scene.
- **Iso (30°):** the figures read, but the poses were designed side-on. From above, a bent digger
  foreshortens into a blob more often than from 10°.

**What would help:**

- The naturalist's photo folders have no pre-contact dress in use, by the nature of photography.
  Drawings or museum reconstructions of people *wearing* cedar-bark skirts and twined capes, in
  motion, would be the best reference I could get. Tribal museum illustrations would be the right
  source, if any are licensed for study.
- Muybridge's plates of women carrying loads on the back, and of kneeling work, at full resolution.
- A decision from the sim on how crowds thin with distance: how many people are drawn at 5 px per
  person.

**Speed:** about 40 ms for one 24 px figure with its ground; 0.06–0.6 s for a whole group scene.
It's pure Python and numpy. The sprites are index canvases, so a sim could bake them per activity ×
phase × facing × size and swap palettes by hour.
"""
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)
EOF
tail -5 LESSONS.md

**Speed:** about 40 ms for one 24 px figure with its ground; 0.06–0.6 s for a whole group scene.
It's pure Python and numpy. The sprites are index canvases, so a sim could bake them per activity ×
phase × facing × size and swap palettes by hour.

Bash: Hem shadow and tuck occlusion
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/people/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
r=open('render.py').read()
old="""    # between people (and between a person and a prop): the farther one takes a dark seam where"""
new="""    # the dark that ties a figure's masses (Bruegel): garments cast a short shadow down onto the legs
    # under the hem, and shadow-family pixels tucked against a nearer part of the same figure go to
    # the core step (under the arm, between the legs, under the chin)
    if size_px >= 12:
        garm = np.isin(cv.part, [p for p, t in cv.tags.items() if t in ('skirt', 'cape', 'blanket', 'dress', 'shirt')])
        limb = np.isin(cv.part, [p for p, t in cv.tags.items() if t[:4] in ('thig', 'shin')])
        hemsh = np.zeros_like(m)
        for d in (1, 2) if size_px >= 20 else (1,):
            above = np.roll(garm, d, 0) & (np.roll(cv.fig, d, 0) == cv.fig)
            hemsh |= limb & above
        step = np.where(hemsh, step - 1, step)
    if size_px >= 16:
        tuck = np.zeros_like(m)
        for dy, dx in ((-1, 0), (1, 0), (0, -1), (0, 1), (-2, 0), (0, -2), (0, 2)):
            zn = np.roll(np.roll(cv.z, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            fn = np.roll(np.roll(cv.fig, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            gn = np.roll(np.roll(cv.group, dy, 0), dx, 1)
            tuck |= (fn == cv.fig) & (gn != cv.group) & (zn > cv.z + 1.2)
        step = np.where(tuck & (step <= 1) & m, 0, step)
    # between people (and between a person and a prop): the farther one takes a dark seam where"""
assert old in r
r=r.replace(old,new)
open('render.py','w').write(r)

import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'.')
import numpy as np
from render import Scene, render_scene, world_pos, smooth_normals
from setting import hearth
from pal import M
sc=Scene(80,50,24,14,ground_y=40)

sc.add_person(dict(activity='sit_log',x=-0.95,z=0.05,facing=5,sex='f',dress='coast_f_wool',seed=1))
wp=world_pos(sc)
n=smooth_normals(sc.cv)
m=(sc.cv.idx//8==M['WOOL'])
fp=np.array([0,0.25,0])
dv=fp-wp; d=np.linalg.norm(dv,axis=-1); dn=dv/d[...,None]
lf=np.maximum((n*dn).sum(-1),0)/(1+(d/0.95)**2)
print('wool px',m.sum(),'lf mean',lf[m].mean().round(3),'max',lf[m].max().round(3),'dist',d[m].mean().round(2))
print('n mean', n[m].mean(0).round(2), 'dn mean', dn[m].mean(0).round(2))

Bash: Wrapped firelight, banded pool
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/people/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
r=open('render.py').read()
old="""            lf = np.maximum((n * dn).sum(-1), 0) / (1 + (dist / reach) ** 2)"""
new="""            # wrapped: firelight is big and soft, it reaches round onto surfaces edge-on to it
            lf = np.maximum(((n * dn).sum(-1) + 0.6) / 1.6, 0) / (1 + (dist / reach) ** 2)"""
assert old in r
r=r.replace(old,new)
# fire pool: noise-broken solid bands instead of checker
old="""    bayer = np.array([[0.125, 0.625], [0.875, 0.375]])[ys % 2, xs % 2]
    lift = np.floor(lift - 0.6 + bayer).astype(int)"""
new="""    # solid bands broken by a little noise (a checker ring read as a net)
    nz = np.random.default_rng(seed + 9).random((H, W))
    lift = np.floor(lift - 0.35 + 0.5 * nz * (nz > 0.8)).astype(int)"""
assert old in r
r=r.replace(old,new)
open('render.py','w').write(r)
EOF

Bash: Two-tone glyph collapse at 8 px and below
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/people/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
r=open('render.py').read()
old="""    step = np.clip(step, 0, NSTEP - 1)
    cv.idx = np.where(m, mat * 8 + step, 0).astype(np.int16)
    return cv.idx"""
new="""    if size_px <= 8:
        # a few pixels tall: collapse each figure to a clean glyph of two tones per material (lit,
        # shadow), no texture, no inner seams; the hair a flat dark; legs keep their far-side step.
        # The seams between people (above) are kept, they are what separates a crowd.
        two = np.where(lam > 0.05, 3, 1)
        legs = np.isin(cv.part, [p for p, t in cv.tags.items() if t[:4] in ('thig', 'shin', 'foot')])
        two = two + np.where(legs, cv.stepmod, 0)
        ishair = (mat == M['HAIR']) | (mat == M.get('HAIRG', -1))
        two = np.where(ishair, 1, two)
        isfig = cv.fig >= 0
        step = np.where(isfig & ~dark2, two, step)
    step = np.clip(step, 0, NSTEP - 1)
    cv.idx = np.where(m, mat * 8 + step, 0).astype(np.int16)
    return cv.idx"""
assert old in r
r=r.replace(old,new)
open('render.py','w').write(r)
EOF
python3 sheets.py F H >/dev/null; echo ok

Bash: Add round 3 to LESSONS
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/people && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('LESSONS.md').read()
r3 = """
## Round 3: the critic again

A second pass by a fresh critic confirmed four fixes: skin, the sand, noon shadows and dig. It found
the rest partly or not fixed, plus three things I had broken. What I changed:

- **The tumpline didn't show.** It ran through the hair sphere, and a depth bias of half a pixel
  couldn't beat it. It now runs from the basket's rim over the top-front of the head, drawn on the
  hair (it lies on it) in pale bark, with a band across the forehead.
- **carry_load still read as red legs**, and then as a red arm when I draped one salmon over the
  shoulder. Now it's three salmon hanging from a carrying pole over the shoulder: an unmistakable
  silhouette at 24 px.
- **The breechclout was still a dark crotch blob** at 24 px. It's now pale twined bark (coast) or
  buckskin (Plateau), a step lighter, with a belt of the same.
- **Sit and sit_eat were one pear.** Sit now leans back with knees up, forearms resting on the
  knees, and the IK reaches past the knee joints. Sit_eat is cross-legged with the bowl on the
  ground in front, one hand in it and one at the mouth.
- **The split log read as a ham.** It's now red-brown bark with a pale split face and the wedge; the
  maul sits in the hand.
- **The rain hat had become a spike (newly broken).** It's now a low wide cone with a dark knob, as
  the guide says.
- **The basket load read as a cabbage.** It's now dark roots.
- **Firelit cloth stayed grey.** Seen from the side, most of a figure's visible surface faces the
  camera, not the fire. Firelight is now wrapped (n·d + 0.6) / 1.6, as a big soft source reaches
  round edges. The glow pool is solid bands broken by noise, not a checker net. Fire is emissive in
  every palette: at dusk it had been dimmer than the blankets.
- **Far scenes still smeared.** Three changes:
  - **A glyph collapse at 8 px and below:** each figure is two tones per material (lit / shadow,
    taken from the resolved light, so it works by firelight too), with flat dark hair, no texture
    and no inner seams. Only the seams between people remain.
  - **Camas diggers spread apart** at small sizes, so there's ground between them.
  - **Weir stakes stay dark.** They had turned tan because props lost their step offsets below
    10 px; now they keep them. The edge rule now applies to people only.
- **Scenery:** the plank house is lower and farther back, and gets a roof band from steeper
  cameras. Gathering baskets are tapered, with a dark rim and the load showing.

![H after round 3](sheets/H_true_scale.png)

At true scale the carrying file at 5–6 px is now a row of clean dots (a dark head, one body colour),
and the canoe is a dark sliver with paddlers as nicks, both as the naturalist describes at 100 m.
"""
s=s.replace("\n---\n\n## Where it stands, honestly\n", r3 + "\n---\n\n## Where it stands, honestly\n")
s=s.replace("| far-limb step and occlusion seam | ≥ 10 px |","| glyph collapse | ≤ 8 px | per figure: two tones per material from the resolved light, flat dark hair, no texture; seams only between people | H sheet, F at 5–8 px |\n| seams between people | all sizes | the farther figure takes a dark edge where a nearer one overlaps it | F sheets |\n| far-limb step and occlusion seam | ≥ 10 px |")
s=s.replace("""- **Seated figures at 12 px and below merge into a blob of skirt and thighs.**""","""- **Seated figures at 12 px and below are compact masses with a dark head.** They read as
  "someone sitting", but the four seated activities are hard to tell apart there.""")
s=s.replace("""- **Far crowds (5 px per person) at real spacing become bands of colour cues** (see Round 2, item
  1).""","""- **Far crowds (5 px per person) at real spacing:** the glyph collapse made a file of walkers
  clean. A packed group of bent diggers is still a band of colour cues; the sim should thin and
  space crowds at distance.""")
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)
EOF
